- 2026-11-25T07:58Z, Phase 2, how the stage is built. (1) Everything that decides what happens is plain TypeScript with no three.js and no DOM: the stage, the cursor's body, every page's layout tree, gags and script, the machine's physics and director. So the same code runs in the browser and in Node, and the bots, the ball path test, the take search, the checkpoints, G12 and G17 run without a browser. (2) A page gives the stage a list of shapes for the moment it is in (each with its part's name, its box in page pixels, its bottom and top above the surface, and its words by key). One blockout view draws that list as flat grey solids with type. A part stops being a stand-in when its own view takes its shapes over. (3) Words are only ever named by key: a copy key, a config key, a key into a data file beside the code (the keyboard's legends, as kit/03-METHOD.md section 7 puts them, and the numerals of the progress bar and the dial), or a run made at run time (typed input, counters, the score display). A shape cannot carry a string written in code, which is what G11 asks. (4) Blockout type is set in Inter from the baked outlines, not in a system face: G11 and M4 read type as geometry, and a system face differs between machines and would break the rule that a computed picture is the same everywhere. (5) A page turn is a half turn out and a half turn in. The slab is edge on at the moment one page ends and the next begins, so every page still starts clean and a film unit can be rendered without the one before it. The cursor jumps to one fixed hand-off place above the page as the slab goes and lands from there.
- 2026-11-25T08:33Z, Phase 2, the contract every part is built on (`src/stage/CONTRACT.md`). (1) Film timing: beat times count from the start of a unit; the first half second of every unit but the first is the page arriving with the cursor in the air at the hand-off place, and the last half second of every unit but the last is the page leaving. In film mode the stage starts the leave at the unit's seconds less half a second whatever the page says, so a unit is always exactly as long as `film.units` gives it. (2) While a page turns only the screen in view is drawn, so a tall page turns as one screen and not as a plank several screens long. (3) A still never shows a page in the middle of a turn: a still whose moment falls in a turn is taken at the nearest moment with the page flat. This moves one judged still, `step3-result`, from the last frame of `pinball.drain` to half a second before the end of the unit. (4) Finishing: the named fallback of kit/03-METHOD.md section 15 is taken for every gag from the start. Each gag has its own way out, and on top of it a plain timer plays the gag's ending once it has run for `live.skipLinkAfterSeconds`. (5) The page state is saved as plain JSON once a simulated second; seeking loads the nearest save and steps on. (6) The cursor's arms are part of its body: a gag gives two places on the page for the hands, and the view draws the arms from the body to them. The body drops the arms by itself outside `cursor.armsOnlyInBeat`. (7) A real opening in the slab is a shape of the kind `well`: the pinball machine's tiers and the ports are wells with their floors below the surface, and the slab is drawn as columns so the walls and the steps between tiers come by themselves. (8) Sheets: the three cursor sheets are made by `src/cursor/sheets.ts`; every other sheet is made by the page that owns it. (9) The button at the end of step 3 is `copy:step2.continue`, because the copy file has no continue of its own for step 3 and may not be edited. (10) The skip link is not offered on the careers page: it has no step to skip.

- 2026-11-25T16:13Z, Phase 3, the page turn is no longer a stand-in (662509e, `src/stage/turn.ts`, five tests in `tests/stage-turn.test.ts`). Inside the half second a page has to leave: it leans back 1.6 degrees over the first 12 percent, then swings away as the 2.2 power of time, so it starts slowly and is fastest edge on. The new page comes in at that same speed, slows, and seats at 60 percent of its half second still moving at a third of the top speed: that is the knock. It rebounds 1.5 degrees, then 0.4 degrees, and lies still for the last 6 percent. No event was added for the knock, so the film's events and the sound table are as they were. Why these numbers: a slab with mass cannot start or stop at once, and a seat that it meets at speed gives a knock where an eased stop gives none. Not yet looked at in a sheet; the shiver of the things on the new page is not built yet, so the part is at its named fallback less the jump, which the cursor's body already does.

  - Section 5, step 1: the quota was read at 17:38:41Z and the ledger started (`audio/CREDITS.md`, written from `audio/CREDITS.json`).
  - The cap is enforced by a tool, not by care: `npm run credits` makes every call. Before a call it adds the call's cost to the running total (at 1 credit a character and 40 credits a second until a rate is measured, then at the measured rate) and makes no call that would pass `audio.creditCap` less `audio.creditReserveAfterReview` (8000 now, 10000 once `--review-reached` is set at the review stop). It refuses a sound effect with no length, asks for no effect shorter than 0.5 s, refuses a call marked `--extra` once the total passes `audio.creditExtrasStopFraction` of the cap, and writes the row before it returns. The cost of a call is the response's own `character-cost` header (MEASURED: both endpoints send it). A call that reports no cost and moves no usage would be counted at the dearest rate. `tests/credits.test.ts` holds 7 tests of these rules. The tool is frozen for every agent.
  - One caller at a time. The Builder made calls 1 to 11 (the tests). From its start the sound agent is the only caller and the ledger on its branch is the count; the Builder makes no call while it lives.
  - Section 5, step 2: the pause and speed tests are run and recorded under "Tool record": the break mark `<break time="N.Ns" />` wins, and every line is spoken at speed 0.7 and stability 0.75. The first effect call measured the effects rate: 12 credits a second.
  - Section 5, step 3, the order of spending: tests (done, 207 credits), one take of every `film` line, one take of every effect (a variant is its own call, so the recipe's "variants are cut apart from one take" is replaced), the `liveOnly` lines, then second and third takes where a first take failed its checks, then extras. At the measured rates one take of everything is about 860 credits of speech and about 1270 of effects (94 calls, 106 s), so about 2350 with the tests: UNKNOWN until made.
  - A sound agent is started now, beside the units, as `kit/08-BUILD-ORDER.md` allows (`audio/`, `public/audio/`). It is the eleventh build agent. While it lives, critics, jurors and render agents together are held to 5 at once, so the total never passes `sandbox.maxAgents` (16). It works in the Builder's own workspace, in its own worktree and branch (`agent/sound`), because the credential and the listening tool are proved to work here and are UNKNOWN in a fresh sandbox, and because its work is network calls and ffmpeg, never a browser render.
  - Section 4, the narrator on the live site: the sound agent builds the rules in `src/audio/` (each `film` line once, on its `live` trigger; never over another line; held back until it can play; dropped when its moment has passed; nothing from a skipped step) and lists every event a page does not report yet. The pages' owners or the Builder add those events. The loader keeps requiring the file's beats to match the config's.
  - Sections 2 and 3: `boss-loop` is made with the sound effect endpoint as a loop, and listed under "For Brandon to decide" if it does not loop cleanly. Extra cues (at most 12), reworded lines (at most 8) and new lines (at most 6) are proposed by the sound agent and decided and logged here by the Builder, old text beside new. Nothing is reworded yet.

- 2026-11-25T20:36Z, a correction of my own words. Statuses 40 and 41 called round 5 "the last one allowed on this unit". That was wrong. The limit `quality.lookLockMaxRounds` (5) counts rounds on `step1`, and rounds 1 and 2 of the build were on the unit `blockout`: round 5 is the third round on `step1`, so two more are allowed by the count. What ends the loop sooner is the clock: an extra round starts only while `npm run clock -- --phase 3` prints `extra rounds: yes`, which it does until 18 h are used under the allowance (about 23:32Z). The fix agents of this round were given 21:00Z as their end because of their own time limit, not because the loop ends there. - 2026-11-25T22:08Z, Phase 3, the fix round after round 5, a fault of M4's reading found by `keys_motion_fixer` and fixed by me in `src/capture/showcase.ts` (`groundAt`). Its fix for R4-2 (the struck key at full travel on the frame of the flash) turned M4 red: H read 1.14 on `step1-fire`, though H is plain to read in the still. Cause: the ground's ray met a triangle of almost no area in the top of the keycap. three.js cannot work out the weights of the hit on such a triangle and gives them as 0, 0, 0 (`Triangle.getBarycoord`: "collinear or singular triangle"), the colour of the corners then read as black, and the measure took a sliver that cannot be seen for a black ground. Now a hit whose weights sum to 0 is passed through and the ray goes on to the surface that can be seen. A test holds it (`tests/gate-measures.test.ts`, "passes a triangle of no area, which has no colour to give"): MEASURED, it fails with the line taken out (ground 0 where 0.8 is right) and passes with it; the file's 23 tests pass. This is not a lowered bar: the threshold and what is counted are unchanged, and a real dark surface still reads dark. Noted in `tools/HARNESS-NOTES.md`.

MEASURED by `tests/cursor-fit.test.ts` (11 tests): no acting state with any reaction is drawn more than a tenth smaller; a flattened arrow is held to between 0.8 and 0.99 of its size (it is a fifth wider than the bore; there is no way to show it wider inside a sealed ball); the reach with the tremble stays inside 41.3 px. ## Notes from Brandon

His notes from the thread, the review rounds and the review, in his words, numbered, each with how it was applied.

1. 2026-11-26T02:26Z, in the thread: "Hey, just wanna give you an update. Everything’s looking good so far. You have my permission to keep working until you’re completely finished with this project. No need to stop. Just keep updating me with your progress along the way". Applied so. a. The build no longer halts at a stop that waits for him. When the clock prints `budget report: due`, the report is written, pushed and shown as a status, and the build takes the continuation that `AGENTS.md` describes for the line `Continue past the budget.`, as often as it falls due. b. At the Phase 5 gate the review is published and its `REVIEW` block is written and shown in full, and the build goes on into Phase 6 as "After the review" in `kit/08-BUILD-ORDER.md` describes, without waiting. Whatever he writes about the review, at any time, is applied as review notes when it arrives. c. Where the clock would close a loop of rounds only because the working time is used up, the Builder takes a continuation first, so that a unit under the pass bar gets the rounds its limit allows. d. Every limit that is not a stop for him stays as the kit sets it: the round limits, the gate's fix attempts, the pass bar, the agents' hours, the status rhythm, the cap of 10,000 sound credits. e. Statuses go on at the same rhythm. This reading is stated in Status 58, so that he can correct it long before the review stop is reached.
2. 2026-11-26T07:19Z, in the thread: "How do you use drawn our tour for recording to put together a lower resolution video to keep track of progress? Would love to see it. Also, if you’re sharing the the output links, you’re not doing it in a way that stays visible to me in a chat where I can access and take a look at your progress. Don’t stop what you’re currently doing, but an update would be nice. More communication, and a rough draft of the video so I can get an idea of what it’s going to look like". Read as four things: make a lower resolution video of the build as it stands and show it; write the links to the site and the review page in the thread's text, where they stay; write more in plain text; do not stop. Applied so. a. A reply went out at about 07:33Z with the state of every unit and the three links as text; from then every status in the thread ends with the links to the site, the review page and the repo. b. Five render agents (`draft1_renderer_1` to `_5`) were started at 07:24Z, each in its own VM, to draw film B of the build at the `draft` tier in the `preview` format (960 by 540, every second film frame) from 9a38f6ba, which is main after merge 27 and before merge 28. They ended between 07:53Z and 08:18Z, every one with exit 0 and the same `engineHash` 08923a79a1f8309e. The pieces were joined on this VM at 08:19Z in 69.3 s (MEASURED from the tool's lines): 4590 frames at 30 a second, 153.00 s, h264 at 4.361 Mbps, with the review mix of the sound (aac, 255 kbps; the mix is 153.000 s, 676 cues, 20 lines of the narrator, -14.02 LUFS, made with no paid call), 83.4 MB. It was looked at in two sheets of frames (17 across the whole film, 16 across the pinball machine) and shown in the thread at 08:24Z as "Rough draft film 1". c. It is not on the review page: `npm run keep -- --film` keeps only a preview that is of the build's own commit, and this one is of 9a38f6ba, so the rule is left as it is and the film is shown in the thread. d. It is made again, numbered, after large merges, as long as render agents are free.

## Open items

Each with its unit, its severity, the still, the critic's words, what was tried and what to try next. A part that ended on a fallback is listed here with the step of the chain that ended it.

- Unit `site`, severity medium. The path for a machine with neither float extension (8 bit targets) is written and not tested: no browser here lacks both extensions, and G10 covers the half float path only. Known risk: the sample target is marked sRGB while the renderer writes linear values, so the post pass may decode twice, and the environment prefilter needs half float. To do in Phase 2 with the engine: force the 8 bit path with a switch, render the spike, and compare it with the float picture by PSNR. Until then the site prints a warning on that path.

Cause 1, found and fixed on the branch: `patchHeights` in `src/post/studio.ts` counted the heights for the shadows along the world's z while the page is turned, so the drop zone's recess read as high ground. On the branch the heights are counted in the page's own frame (a uniform `wjaPage` added to the uniforms of every `THREE.ShaderLib` entry, set by `setPageFrame` from `src/shell/view.ts` after the pivot is turned; `aim()` and `seen()` in `src/core/renderer.ts` fitted to the turned page; the identity at rest), and the fill light's shadows fade with the turn (`lightLaw.fillShadow`, gone at 4 degrees). With it the crease across the turning page at frame 850 and the black bowtie at frame 2746 are gone. Cause 2, not found: with the same code frame 850 is clean when it is the first frame drawn in a session, and it carries two grey wedges on the glass card (the left and right fan triangles of its cap) when it is drawn after another frame, so something is carried over from the frame before. Not checked on the branch: that a frame at rest is the same picture as main's, the measures, and a change to the slab's own up in `src/shell/materials.ts` that rode along. The rule's shimmer was not looked at. To try next, beside Phase 4's work because every page turns: draw frame 849 and then 850 in one session and switch off one pass at a time (the height pass, the id pass, the shadow maps, anything kept between frames) until 850 is the picture it is alone; prove a frame at rest identical to main's; run the measures; then merge. ## For Brandon to decide

- The approved images were made with `seedream_5`. Its maker's terms do not clearly allow outputs in a published video or on a public website, and they do not forbid it. The workspace's list of third-party models has no row for that maker, so which terms reach those pictures is unknown. The build uses the approved images as the kit says. Nothing waits on this. Details: `ASSETS.md`, "Generated images".
- The free plan of `host.name` is worded for personal or non-commercial use. Whether this portfolio piece fits that is for you to judge before it goes live there. Details: `references/terms/hosting-vercel.md`, section 6.
- A permanent public address for the review build. The workspace's hosting keeps the published site private to you. If you want it public before it goes to `host.name`, name the address you want in the thread and it will be published there.
- The page's grain in the tug's close frame. The sharpness measure passes there only with a fine tooth on the page that was fitted to that camera distance (0.32 without it, 0.94 with it, least 0.8), and the tooth is absent on the closer cursor sheets, whose approved pages are smooth. The critics of round 4 wanted the page smoother in that frame. Say if the tooth should go (the measure then fails at that key and is logged red), stay, or be the same at every close distance. Details: the decision of 2026-11-25T20:28Z and `pipeline/phase3/surface.md`, "Round 5".
- Step 1's Continue while nothing is filled in. In the build it waits as a smoked grey button until the resume is received and then turns orange. `kit/02-SUBJECT.md` says that for step 2 ("a Continue button that stays grey until all three are done") and says nothing for step 1; the approved `page-look.png` shows the main button orange on an empty form. The judged page-look sheet now shows the button open so that it is compared with the approved image. Say if step 1's Continue should be orange from the start. Nothing waits on this.
- The orange. The approved pages show a brighter orange than the build's: the approved hero button has a median of (204, 93, 34), the build's about (168, 72, 30). `layout.minTextContrast` is 4.5 and M4 computes it from the materials' colours; white type on the approved orange measures 3.77 by pixels, so the approved brightness and 4.5 cannot both hold, and the build holds 4.5 (4.57). Say if the orange may go to the approved brightness with the contrast limit lowered for type on orange, or stay. Details: the decision of 2026-11-26T02:05Z.
- The careers page's nav button. The approved `marketing-top` draws "Apply Now" in the header smaller than the hero button. The build draws it as the shared button, 72 high, because "One scale" in `kit/02-SUBJECT.md` gives one size to every button and takes no size from an image. Say if the header's button should have a smaller size of its own; it is one number in `scale.sizes` and one line on the page.
- The captions of the sheet `boss-stages`. The approved sheet prints a word under each stage (Resting, Breaking loose, Unfolding, Boss). `content/copy.json` has no such strings and no word may be taken from an image, so the build's sheet shows the four stages with no captions, and no page of the site is touched by it. Say if you want captions on the sheet, and give the words; they would go into the copy as you write them.
- The header on the boss page. `kit/02-SUBJECT.md` and `kit/03-METHOD.md` make the boss's two cannons out of the numbered buttons of the progress row ("the cannons fly in from the header and dock"), and the settled line of the sheet `boss-stages` says so too, so from the unfolding to the collapse the build's header shows buttons 3, 4 and 5 and a bare rail where 1 and 2 were. The approved `boss-fight` and `boss-down` draw all five buttons in the header. The build follows the words. A critic who sets the key beside the approved image raises it each round, and it is declined each time, because only you add a settled line. Say if the two keys should carry such a line, or if the header should keep all five.
- The tier views of the machine. `kit/03-METHOD.md` makes the three tier cameras the `home` camera moved down the page to the tier, looking straight down with one pixel of the floor on one screen pixel, so `tier-corporate`, `tier-office` and `tier-street` read as plans; the approved tier images look down at a slant, with the fronts of the models seen and the field nearly wall to wall. The build follows the kit's rule. Say if the tier cameras should take the approved slant.
- The steps between the machine's floors. `table.tierStepBalls` and `table.topFloorDepthBalls` are 1 in the kit, so each floor of the machine lies one ball below the floor behind it and the cabinet's walls stand tall round them. The approved `table-wide` and `table-above` show one shallow cabinet whose floors read as nearly level, with thin bright rails, and round 11's critics score that difference hardest. The numbers are the kit's, so the build keeps them and closes what it can with wall, rail and camera. Say if the steps should be smaller, and by how much.
- The arrow flattened by other hits. In the kit the damage `flattened` belongs to the doors of Corporate HQ. Round 11's acting critics ask for the arrow to show harm at other hits of the machine too (bumpers, slings, flippers). The build answers every hit with a pose in the ball, a squash and a recoil, and raises the damage at the doors alone. Say if another hit should flatten it.
- The Continue that is not yet live. The approved pages draw it as a pale pill whose label has less contrast than `layout.minTextContrast` (4.5). The build keeps 4.5 on every label (M4 MEASURED 4.62 on the shared look's branch, 6983a6b2), so its waiting Continue is darker than approved. Say if a button that is not live may fall under 4.5.
- The orange cap of the progress row. In the approved pages the cap of the current step is larger than the steel caps and throws a long soft shadow (MEASURED by `look_fixer`: still at 0.8 of its depth 42 to 56 px past the cap, where the build's shadow is over at 34 px). The build draws it at the size of the others, because the sizes come from the kit's one scale, and with a short shadow, because `look.shadowHeights.keep` is 0.2 and also holds the flying arrow's shadow close; raising it moves the shadow of every tall thing on every page. The build keeps both numbers. Say if the current cap should have a size of its own or if `look.shadowHeights.keep` should rise.
- The card at the foot of the machine in the comparison of `table-wide`. The settled line of `table-wide` keeps the build's words on the apron (the HR card) where the approved image has words on the marquee and the drain plate alone, so the compare tool's crop named for the apron card shows the build's HR card beside the image's drain plate, and critics of rounds 11 and 12 read that pair as a fault. The build follows the settled line and the tools stay as they are inside the unit's rounds. Say if the crop should pair the two drain plates instead.
- The length of the shadows on the pages. The craft critics of rounds 13 and 14 name the build's cast shadows as shorter and weaker than the approved pages' (round 13 R1-1 issue 6: "in the target the key light from the upper left throws long, soft shadows down and to the right"; R1-2 issues 3, 4 and 6; round 14 R1-1 issues 3, 4 and 17). Their length comes from the Look lock's `look.shadowHeights`, whose `keep` is 0.2 in the kit, and a number the kit sets is yours to change. The build keeps it and no owner lengthens a shadow by another road. Say if the shadows may grow toward the approved images' length; every page takes it from the one setting.
- The two answered fields above the crank. In the film's order the dial and the slot machine are done before the crank opens, so in the key `step2-crank` the fields of Contact number and Date of birth carry the copy's lines ("Close enough. We were never going to call." and "Date of birth recorded. Corrections are not supported."), where the approved image shows both wells empty. The settled line of `step2-crank` covers the Audio volume field alone. Round 16's craft critic names it as severe (R1-2 issue 1). The build keeps the lines, since the film's order and the copy make them; say if the key should show the two wells empty, or add a settled line.
